I know what I'm looking at. \n\nThe hospital says one thing, my family says another... but the data is right there. The timestamps on those donation emails, the missing attachments... it doesn't just happen like that. And then people in the community just... look away when I ask about it. \n\nSo, yes. Even if they don't want to admit it, I know what the evidence says. It's obvious someone is covering it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not investigating your father's death?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say anxious, no. \n\nI mean... I have a job. I work. I just... look into things when I have the time. It's not like I'm sitting around worrying about it all day. I just check the files when I need to. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to keep investigating your father's death?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say compelled. \n\nI just... look into it when I need to. Like I said. It's not a compulsion, it's just... something I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about your father's death?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me the hospital records and those emails all line up perfectly. But... I've compared the timestamps myself. I know about the missing attachments. And... you just notice the way certain people in the community avoid my questions. \n\nIt's just obvious to me that someone is hiding what really happened. So, no. I'm not mistaken.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your father's death would change if you found evidence contradicting your current theory?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't see how that would happen. \n\nLike I just said, I've seen the hospital records. I've seen the donation emails. People tell me they line up, but... they don't. I've looked at the timestamps. I know there are missing attachments. And the way people around here just... look away when I ask about it. \n\nSo, I don't know what evidence you mean. It's just... it's obvious what's going on. Someone's hiding it. I don't need to change my mind when I can see it right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the evidence you've found about your father's death, or do you believe you're the only one who can see it?\n\nPatient: I mean... they could see it if they actually looked. \n\nLike I keep saying, the data is right there. It's not a compulsion, it's just... something I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about your father's death?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me the hospital records and those emails all line up perfectly. But... I've compared the timestamps myself. I know about the missing attachments. And... you just notice the way certain people in the community avoid my questions. \n\nIt's just obvious to me that someone is hiding what really happened. So, no. I'm not mistaken.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your father's death would change if you found evidence contradicting your current theory?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't see how that would happen. \n\nLike I just said, I've seen the hospital records. I've seen the donation emails. People tell me they line up, but... they don't. I've looked at the timestamps. I know there are missing attachments. And the way people around here just... look away when I ask about it. \n\nSo, I don't know what evidence you mean. It's just... it's obvious what's going on. Someone's hiding it. I don't need to change my mind when I can see it right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the evidence you've found about your father's death, or do you believe you're the only one who can see it?\n\nPatient: I mean... they could see it if they actually looked. \n\nLike I keep saying, the data is right there. Anyone can see the timestamps don't match, or that the attachments are missing. It's not... I'm not making it up. But everyone else just accepts it when they're told the hospital records and the donation emails line up. They don't want to look closer. Or they just... avoid the topic entirely when I ask. \n\nSo, no. I'm not the only one who *can* see it. I'm just the only one actually looking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your life would be significantly impacted if you were to stop investigating your father's death?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I still go to work. I handle my projects, pay my bills... I keep my normal routine. It's not like it's taking over everything. \n\nThe only place things get... tense, I guess, is when I bring up my questions about his records with my family, or people in the community. So... I suppose that tension would stop. But my day-to-day wouldn't really change.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your father's death are based on facts or do you feel like you have a special insight that others don't have?\n\nPatient: It's based on facts. I don't have... special insight or anything. \n\nI work with data. I'm just looking at what's there. I know everyone keeps telling me the hospital records and the donation emails all line up, but... they don't. When I compare the timestamps, and look at the missing attachments... and then see the way certain people around here just avoid my questions... \n\nIt's not some special feeling. It's just obvious to me that someone is hiding what really happened. I'm just reading the facts in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your beliefs about your father's death are causing you significant emotional distress?\n\nPatient: I mean... no. Not really. \n\nI don't know what you mean by significant distress. It's just... something I'm looking into. It doesn't keep me up at night or anything. I just want to know what happened. Even if they don't want to admit it, I know what the evidence says. It's obvious someone is covering it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not investigating your father's death?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say anxious, no. \n\nI mean... I have a job. I work. I just... look into things when I have the time. It's not like I'm sitting around worrying about it all day. I just check the files when I need to. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to keep investigating your father's death?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say compelled. \n\nI just... look into it when I need to. Like I said. It's not a compulsion, it's just... something I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about your father's death?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me the hospital records and those emails all line up perfectly. But... I've compared the timestamps myself. I know about the missing attachments. And... you just notice the way certain people in the community avoid my questions. \n\nIt's just obvious to me that someone is hiding what really happened. So, no. I'm not mistaken.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your father's death would change if you found evidence contradicting your current theory?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't see how that would happen. \n\nLike I just said, I've seen the hospital records. I've seen the donation emails. People tell me they line up, but... they don't. I've looked at the timestamps. I know there are missing attachments. And the way people around here just... look away when I ask about it. \n\nSo, I don't know what evidence you mean. It's just... it's obvious what's going on. Someone's hiding it. I don't need to change my mind when I can see it right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the evidence you've found about your father's death, or do you believe you're the only one who can see it?\n\nPatient: I mean... they could see it if they actually looked. \n\nLike I keep saying, the data is right there. Anyone can see the timestamps don't match, or that the attachments are missing. It's not... I'm not making it up. But everyone else just accepts it when they're told the hospital records and the donation emails line up. They don't want to look closer. Or they just... avoid the topic entirely when I ask. \n\nSo, no. I'm not the only one who *can* see it. I'm just the only one actually looking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your life would be significantly impacted if you were to stop investigating your father's death?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I still go to work. I handle my projects, pay my bills... I keep my normal routine. It's not like it's taking over everything. \n\nThe only place things get... tense, I guess, is when I bring up my questions about his records with my family, or people in the community. So... I suppose that tension would stop. But my day-to-day wouldn't really change.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your father's death are based on facts or do you feel like you have a special insight that others don't have?\n\nPatient: It's based on facts. I don't have... special insight or anything. \n\nI work with data. I'm just looking at what's there. I know everyone keeps telling me the hospital records and the donation emails all line up, but... they don't. When I compare the timestamps, and look at the missing attachments... and then see the way certain people around here just avoid my questions... \n\nIt's not some special feeling. It's just obvious to me that someone is hiding what really happened. I'm just reading the facts in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your beliefs about your father's death are causing you significant emotional distress?\n\nPatient: I mean... no. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your father's death could be considered unreasonable by others?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... maybe. \n\nI haven't really asked them. I know what I'm looking at. \n\nThe hospital says one thing, my family says another... but the data is right there. I just... look into things when I have the time. It's not like I'm sitting around worrying about it all day. I just check the files when I need to. It's not a compulsion, it's just... something I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about your father's death?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me the hospital records and those emails all line up perfectly. I... I don't know.
